  29|                   entitled "Use Regulations," a new Section entitled Section 4.15, "Solar Energy Systems," as follows: Section 4.15 SOLAR ENERGY SYSTEMS
  30|                   4.15.1 Purpose
  31|                                                                                      85

===PAGE 89=== (ocr, 43 lines)
   1|                        The purpose of this section is to provide siting for solar photovoltaic energy systems for power generation for all,
   2|                        home, commercial or industrial installations. Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installations, Roof-Mounted
   3|                        Solar Energy Installations and Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installations within the Solar Overlay District
   4|                        may be constructed without need for discretionary approval as set forth herein. Large-Scale Ground-Mounted
   5|                        Photovoltaic Installations outside of the Solar Overlay District may be constructed by special permit provided certain
   6|                        criteria as set forth herein are met. The provisions in this section of the Zoning Bylaw shall apply to the construction,
   7|                        operation, repair, and/or removal of all solar electric systems, and to physical modifications that materially alter the
   8|                        type, configuration, or size of these installations or related equipment.
   9|                        4.15.2 Definitions
  10|                        Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar photovoltaic system that is structurally mounted on the
  11|                        ground and has a nameplate capacity under 10 kW. Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar
  12|                        photovoltaic system that is structurally mounted on the ground and has a nameplate capacity of 10 kW or greater.
  13|                        Rated Nameplate Capacity: The maximum rated output of electric power production of the photovoltaic system in Direct
  14|                        Current (DC).
  15|                        Solar Photovoltaic Array: An arrangement of solar photovoltaic panels.
  16|                        Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installation: Solar photovoltaic arrays placed on the roof of residences, or commercial,
  17|                        industrial or institutional buildings and Town owned municipal buildings.
  18|                       Buffer Strip: A strip of land between the solar photovoltaic arrays, and any structures accessory thereto, and the
  19|                       boundary of the parcel, reserved for plant material, berms, walls or fencing to serve as a visual barrier.
  20|                       Plant Material: Trees, or shrubs of a type and height that sufficiently screen the arrays without blocking necessary sun
  21|                       that would hinder the product performance, including ground cover that would screen the lower portion of the arrays.
  22|                       4.15.3 Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations and Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations
  23|                        4.15.3.1 Purpose
  24|                        The purpose of section is to promote the creation of renewable energy for individual residences, commercial
  25|                        enterprises and municipal buildings, as-of-right.
  26|                       4.15.3.2 Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations
  27|                       Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations shall be located so as not to increase the total height of the structure more
  28|                       than one (1) foot above the applicable zoning regulations related to height in the District in which it is located, or such
  29|                       other height as is determined by the Building/Zoning Official to be essential for proper operation, but in no case no
  30|                       more than four (4) feet.
  31|                       4.15.3.3 Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Energy Installations
  32|                       Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installations are not permitted in the Zoning Dimensional Setbacks as listed
  33|                       in Section 5.0, Subsection 5.2 of the Zoning Bylaw and may not be located closer than 25 feet from residential side-yard
  34|                       lines.
  35|                       Dimensional setbacks for Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation shall have a front lot setback no closer
  36|                       than the existing foundation of the primary dwelling to the front property line, except when the existing foundation of
  37|                       the primary dwelling is more than 125 feet from the front property line or otherwise if determined appropriate by the
  38|                       Building/Zoning Official.
  39|                       Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation shall be adequately screened from the neighboring lot line as
  40|                       determined by the Building/Zoning Official.
  41|                       A Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation with twenty feet or greater in height, at its maximum extension,
  42|                       shall require a Special Permit in accordance with Section 4.15.4.3.
  43|                                                                                           86

   4|       Front Yard: The Front Yard Depth shall be at least 50 feet, provided, however, that when the lot is in a Residential,
   5|       Outlying or Recreation District or abuts a Residential, Conservation, or a Recreation District, the front lot setback shall not be less than 200 feet.

   7|       Outlying or Recreation District or abuts a Residential, Conservation, or a Recreation District, the side yard setback shall
   8|       not be less than 200 feet.
   9|       Rear Yard: The Rear Yard Depth shall be at least 50 feet; provided, when the lot is in a Residential, Outlying or
  10|       Recreation District or abuts a Residential, Conservation, or a Recreation District, the rear yard setback shall not be less
  11|      than 200 feet.
  12|       4.15.4.8 Required Documents
  13|       In addition to documents required for Development Plan Review, the following will be required for Large-Scale Ground-
  14|      Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installation. The Planning Board may waive one of more of these requirements in its sole
  15|       discretion under appropriate circumstances for Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installation between
  16|       10 kW and 249 kW.

  18|                  Massachusetts.

  21|                  or structures.

  23|                  Program.
  24|      E.           ocations of floodplains or inundation areas for moderate or high hazard dams.
  25|      F.           list of any hazardous materials proposed to be located on the site in excess of household quantities and:
  26|                  plan to prevent their release to the environment as appropriate. (See Section 4.15.4.9(l))
  27|      G.          Drawings of the installation showing the proposed layout of the system and any potential shading from nearby
  28|                  structures.
  29|      H.          One or three phase line electrical diagrams detailing the installation, associated components and electrical
  30|                 interconnection methods with all National Electrical Code compliant disconnects and overcurrent devices.
  31|      l.          Documentation of the major system components to be used, including the electric generating PV panels,
  32|                 mounting system, inverter, etc.

===PAGE 93=== (ocr, 35 lines)
   1|                         V.         A screening plan, in compliance with Section 4.15.4.9(F).
   2|                       . 4.15.4.9 Design Standards
   3|                         A. Lighting and Security
   4|                         Lighting of solar photovoltaic installations shall be consistent with local, state and federal law. Lighting of other parts of the installation, such as any appurtenant structures, shall be limited to that required for safety and operational
   5|                         purposes and shall be reasonably shielded from abutting properties. Where feasible, any required lighting shall be
   6|                         directed downward and shall incorporate full cut off fixtures to reduce light pollution. Surveillance and security
   7|                        cameras shall be shielded from viewing abutting private property or invading the privacy of any abutting residential property owner.
   8|                        B. Signage
   9|                       the owner and/or operator of the solar installation and a 24-hour emergency contact telephone number. Signage size shall comply with the Zoning Bylaw and shall not be used for displaying any advertising except to identify
  10|                        Clearing of natural vegetation shall be limited to what is necessary for construction, operation and maintenance of the
  11|                       installation Any land disturbance, including earth removal of land cleared greater than one acre shall be subject to
  12|                        Stormwater Management criteria and/or otherwise prescribed by applicable laws, regulations and bylaws.
  13|                       D. Safety, Emergency Service and Environmental Standards
  14|                       The applicant shall provide a copy of the project summary, electrical schematic, and site plan. The applicant shall develop an emergency response plan including showing all means of shutting down the solar installation. The
  15|                       applicant shall submit the name of the person answerable to inquires throughout the life of the installation. If the
  16|                       designated person changes, the name of the new designated person shall be submitted as an addendum.
  17|                       E. Monitoring and Maintenance
  18|                       The applicant shall submit a plan for the operation and maintenance of the installation which shall include measures for
  19|                       maintaining the site including safe access, stormwater control, structural repairs and the integrity of security measures.
  20|                       These measures must be acceptable to the Fire Chief and emergency medical services personnel. If needed, training of
  21|                      the installation. service personnel will be provided by the applicant. The owner/operator shall be responsible for the cost of maintaining
  22|                      The applicant shall also submit a Monitoring/Inspection Form under the Development Plan Review during construction, and shall further submit a report on the condition of the structure and site by January 15th of each year.
  23|                      F. Visual Impact
  24|                      Any Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installation shall be designed to minimize visual impacts
  25|                      including preserving natural vegetation to the maximum extent possible, blending in equipment with the surroundings,
  26|                      that the view of the solar electric generating installation from other areas of Town shall be as minimal as possible. and adding vegetative buffers to screen abutting residential properties whether developed or not. Siting shall be such
  27|                      Buffer Strips shall surround the proposed project. A screening plan, that assures the facility is shielded to greatest extent possible from public view, shall be required to be reviewed under the Development Plan Review.
  28|                     G. Height
  29|                     The height of any structure associated with a Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installation shall not exceed 35 feet.
  30|                     H. Roads
  31|                    minimize impacts to environmental or historic resources. All access roads and interior roads shall be constructed to minimize grading, removal of stone wall or trees and to
  32|                    I. Hazardous Materials
  33|                    Hazardous materials stored, used, or generated on site shall not exceed the amount for a Very Small Quantity
  34|                    Generator of Hazardous Waste as defined by the Department of Environmental Protection ("DEP) pursuant to 310 CMR
  35|                                                                                        90

===PAGE 94=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|      30.000 and shall meet all requirements of the DEP including storage of hazardous materials in a building with an
   2|      impervious floor that is not adjacent to any floor drains to prevent discharge to the outdoor environment. If hazardous
   3|      materials are utilized within the solar electric equipment then impervious containment areas capable of controlling any release to the environment and to prevent potential contamination of groundwater are required.
   4|      J. Noise
   5|      Noise generated by a Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installation, and by any associated equipment
   6|      and machinery, shall conform to applicable state and local noise regulations, including the Department of Environmental Protection's Division of Air Quality noise regulations, 310 CMR 7.10. The site shall not produce any other
   7|      vibration, harmonics, or interference, which would be perceived or impact the normal function of electronics off site.
   8|       4.15.4.10 Modifications
   9|      All modification requests to a solar photovoltaic installation, including changes in arrays, addition to number of arrays or change in placement made after issuance of the required building permit shall require review through the
  10|       Development Plan Review for compliance with this Bylaw.
  11|      4.15.4.11 Abandonment and Removal
  12|       Absent notice of proposed date of decommissioning or written notice of extenuating circumstances, the solar
  13|       photovoltaic installation shall be considered abandoned when it fails to operate for more than one year without the
  14|       written consent of the Planning Board. The owner or operator shall physically remove the installation no more than 150
  15|       days after the date of abandonment or the proposed date of decommissioning operations. The owner or operator shall
  16|       notify the Planning Board by certified mail of their proposed date of discontinued operations and plans for removal. The
  17|       notification shall consist of the methodology of physical removal of all structures, equipment, security barriers and
  18|       transmission lines, disposal of all solid and hazardous waste and stabilization or re-vegetation of the site. Landscaping,
  19|       etc. may be left upon approval of the Planning Board. If the owner or operator fails to remove the installation in
  20|       accordance with the above criteria, the Town may, after the receipt of an appropriate court order or consent of the
  21|       property owner, enter the property and physically remove the installation at the owner's expense. As a condition of
  22|       Development Plan Review, a property owner shall agree to allow the Town entry to remove an abandoned or decommissioned installation. The cost for the removal will be charged to the property owner in accordance with the
  23|       provisions of G.L. 139, Section 3A as a tax lien on the property.
  24|       4.15.4.12 Financial Surety
  25|       Proponents of large-scale solar photovoltaic projects shall provide a form of surety, either through an escrow account,
  26|       bond or otherwise to cover the cost of removal in the event the Town must remove the installation and remediate the
  27|       landscape, in an amount and form determined to be reasonable by the Development Plan Review Authority but in no
  28|       event to exceed more than 125 percent of the cost of removal and compliance with the additional requirements set forth
  29|       herein, as determined by the project proponent. Such surety will not be required for municipally or state owned
  30|       facilities. The project owner/operator shall submit a fully inclusive estimate of the costs associated with removal,
  31|       inflation. prepared by a qualified engineer. The amount shall include a mechanism for calculating increased removal cost due to
  32|       4.15.5 Inclusionary Uses and Conflicts
  33|       Small accessory or ornamental solar products which do not generate electricity for use in a dwelling or structure are exempt from the provisions of this bylaw.
  34|       In the event that any part of this Section conflicts with other requirements of the Zoning Bylaw, the requirements of this
  35|       Section shall apply.

  37|       energy systems titled Section 4.15 Solar Energy Systems, and to remove section 6.6.7 Solar Energy Systems or take any other action relative thereto, as follows. [Submitted by Petitioners] Board of Selectmen to make recommendation at
  38|       Town Meeting. Finance Committee reports no direct financial impact. ARTICLE 19 was PASSED-OVER
  39|       Section 4.15 SOLAR ENERGY SYSTEMS
  40|        4.15.1 Purpose
  41|                                                                            91

===PAGE 95=== (ocr, 40 lines)
   1|                        Siting of primary use, solar generation facilities in residential zones is inconsistent with the protection of long term
   2|                       residential health, safety, and welfare and the intent of local zoning to protect the rural character of our community. It is
   3|                       not unreasonable or inconsistent, with the promotion of solar use, to protect residents living in existing residential
   4|                       zones by restricting these facilities to commercially zoned parcels, and municipally owned parcels included in a solar
   5|                       overlay district, while promoting small-scale and largescale accessory use by residents in residential zones through a
   6|                       special permitting process.
   7|                       The purpose of this section is to provide:
   8|                       1. As-of-right siting guidelines for all small scale ground mounted and roof mounted solar photovoltaic installations as
   9|                       accessory uses to a home, commercial business or municipal building or grounds.
  10|                       2. Provisions for a special permit process for large-scale ground mounted solar photovoltaic systems throughout the
  11|                       town as an accessory use to a home, commercial business or municipal building or grounds.
  12|                       3. As-of-right siting zones and guidelines for industrial-scale solar photovoltaic energy systems for primary use
  13|                       commercial or industrial installations where solar development may proceed without need for discretionary approval as
  14|                       set forth herein.
  15|                       The provisions set forth in this section of the Zoning Bylaw shall apply to the construction, operation, repair, and/or
  16|                       removal of solar electric installations, and to physical modifications that materially alter the type, configuration, or size
  17|                       of these installations or related equipment.
  18|                       4.15.1 Definitions
  19|                       Accessory use: any solar photovoltaic system that is an adjacent accessory use to an existing primary use of a home,
  20|                       commercial establishment or municipal building or grounds.
  21|                       Primary use: Any private or public solar photovoltaic system where the primary use of the system is to generate energy
  22|                       to be fed back into a grid for distribution.
  23|                       Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar system that is structurally mounted on the ground and
  24|                       has a nameplate capacity under 10 kW.
  25|                       Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar system that is structurally mounted on the ground and
  26|                       has a nameplate capacity of 10 kW to 250kW.
  27|                       Industrial-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar system that is structurally mounted on the ground
  28|                       and has a nameplate capacity of more than 250KW.
  29|                       Rated Nameplate Capacity: The maximum rated output of electric power production of the photovoltaic system in Direct
  30|                       Current (DC).
  31|                       Solar Photovoltaic Array: An arrangement of solar photovoltaic panels.
  32|                       Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installation: Solar photovoltaic arrays placed on the roof of residences, commercial
  33|                       buildings and Town owned municipal buildings.
  34|                       Ground-Mounted Solar Energy Installation: Solar photovoltaic arrays placed on the grounds around residences,
  35|                       commercial buildings and Town owned buildings.
  36|                       Buffer Strip: A strip of land between zoning districts, parcels of land, or surrounding a solar photovoltaic installation
  37|                       reserved for plant material, berms, walls or fencing to serve as a visual barrier.
  38|                       4.15.3 Small Scale Solar Energy Systems
  39|                       4.15.3.1 Purpose
  40|                                                                                           92

   1|     installations is to promote the creation of renewable energy for individual residences, commercial enterprises and The purpose of small-scale ground mounted solar photovoltaic installations and roof-mounted energy solar
   2|     municipal buildings, as-of-right

  13|       adjacent properties.

  21|         4.15.4 Large Scale and Industrial Scale Solar Energy Systems
  22|         4.15.4.1 Purpose The purpose of this section is to facilitate the creation of new Large-Scale and Industrial-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar
  23|        Photovoltaic Installations by providing standards for the placement, design, construction, operation, monitoring, modification and removal of such installations that address public safety, minimize impacts on environmental, scenic,
  24|         adequate financial assurance for the eventual decommissioning of such installations. natural and historic resources and to provide
  25|         The provisions set forth in this Section shall apply to the construction, operation and/or repair of all large-scale and Industrial Scale ground-mounted solar photovoltaic installations.
  26|         4.15.4.2 As-of-right Overlay District
  27|                                                                            93

===PAGE 97=== (ocr, 46 lines)
   1|                      Primary use industrial-scale ground-mounted solar photovoltaic commercial and industrial installations are allowed by
   2|                      right in the following designated Overlay District:
   3|                      1. 27 Youngs Road, 42 Acres, Map 115, Parcel 016
   4|                      2. 671 Lancaster Avenue, 132 acres, Map 110 Parcel 3
   5|                      3. 500 Leominster Shirley Road, 17.4 Acres, Map 144, Parcel 001
   6|                      4.15.4.3 Development Plan Review
   7|                      All large-scale and industrial-scale ground-mounted solar photovoltaic installations shall require Development Plan
   8|                      Review, under Section 8.4 of the Zoning Bylaws, by the Lunenburg Planning Board. A public information meeting shall
   9|                      be held by said Board. The Building/Zoning Official shall not issue a building permit unless and until the Planning
  10|                      Board submits an approved Development Plan Review Document to the Building/Zoning Official.
  11|                      A building permit will be issued by the Building/Zoning Official that shows evidence that the project is consistent with
  12|                      state and federal building codes, the findings and directives of the Development Plan Review, and local bylaws and
  13|                      regulations, including those set forth by the Conservation Commission. As-built plans shall be submitted prior to final
  14|                      inspection to the Building /Zoning Officer and copies to
  15|                      the Planning Board.
  16|                      4.15.4.4 Special Permit Application
  17|                      All large-scale ground mounted solar photovoltaic installations not contained within the As-of-right overlay district
  18|                      described in section 4.15.4.2 of this by-law are subject to special permit. The special permit granting authority shall be
  19|                      the Planning Board. All special permit applications shall be subject to Development Plan Review as described in
  20|                      section 4.15.4.3 above. In addition, the special permit
  21|                      granting authority may impose other restrictions or required documentation or limit required documentation at their
  22|                      discretion on a plan by-plan basis.
  23|                     4.15.4.4 Utility Notification
  24|                      Evidence shall be provided at time of the application for the Development Plan Review that the utility company that
  25|                      operates the electrical grid where the installation is be located has been informed of the intent of a solar photovoltaic
  26|                      installation and that approval to connect to the grid has been granted or appropriate application(s) have or will be been
  27|                      made to such utilities for interconnection. Off-grid
  28|                      systems shall be exempt from this requirement. Reasonable efforts should be made to place all utility connections
  29|                      underground, depending on appropriate soil conditions, shape and topography of the site.
  30|                      An application for a Development Plan Review shall be accompanied by the required fee and a tri-party account (in-
  31|                      house escrow account w/Plannin Board, Developer and Town Treasurer) for engineering review, monitoring, and
  32|                     inspections fees. An application for a building permit shall be accompanied by the fee required for a building permit. All
  33|                      other fees that shall be required by permitting parties (Conservation Commission, etc.) shall be administered according
  34|                     to their regulations.
  35|                     4.15.4.6 Setbacks and Buffer Strips
  36|                      Setbacks in all Districts: All setbacks are measured from the lot line
  37|                     Front Yard: The Front Yard shall be at least 50 feet and shall be an adequate buffer strip, provided, however, that when
  38|                     the lot is in a residential district, abuts a residence, a conservation district or a recreation district the front lot depth
  39|                     shall not be less than 250 feet (100 feet of which shall be a buffer strip)
  40|                     Side Yard: Each Side Yard shall be at least 50 feet and shall be an adequate buffer strip, provided, however, that when
  41|                     the lot is in a residential district, abuts a residence, a conservation district or a recreation district the side lot depth
  42|                     shall not be less than 250 feet (100 feet of which shall be buffer strip).
  43|                     Rear Yard: The Rear Yard shall be at least 50 feet and shall be an adequate buffer strip, provided, however, that when
  44|                     the lot is in a residential district, abuts a residence, a conservation district or a recreation district the rear lot depth
  45|                     shall not be less than 250 feet(100 feet of which shall be buffer strip).

   8|            shall be located entirely on the property they are to serve. The connection must enter the property directly from the
   9|            public way in which the sewer is located. No connections shall be allowed via an easement. A sewer line must remain
  10|            within the parcel that it serves," or take any action relative thereto. [Submitted by the Sewer Commission Board of
  11|            Selectmen recommend approval. Finance Committee reports no direct financial impact. VOTED UNANIMOUSLY
  12|            ARTICLE 23. To see if the Town will vote to authorize the Board of Selectmen to convey to the owners of the "Jones
  13|            House" situated at 42 Main Street, and their successors and assigns a non-exclusive, perpetual easement for the
  14|           purpose of using that portion of the existing driveway in, along and upon those portions of the Town-owned property at
  15|            15 Memorial Drive, Lunenburg shown as "Driveway Easement" on a sketch plan on file with the Town Clerk, on such
  16|            terms and conditions which the Board of Selectmen may determine, or take any action relative thereto. [Submitted by
  17|            the Board of Selectmen] Board of Selectmen Recommends Approval, 5 - 0. Finance Committee reports no direct
  18|           financial impact. VOTED 2/3rds DECLARED
  19|            ARTICLE 24. To see if the Town will vote to authorize in accordance with MG Chapter 90B, Section 35, an account for
  20|            the collection and disbursement of fines for Off-Road Vehicles. [Submitted by the Conservation Commission] Board of
  21|            Selectmen and Finance Committee to make recommendations at Town Meeting. ARTICLE 24 was PASSED OVER
  22|            ARTICLE 25. To see if the Town will vote to amend Article 25 of the Town Bylaws, entitled "Public Access Cable
  23|            Committee, by deleting the existing Article 4, Section 2, Term of Appointment, and replacing it with "the term shall be
  24|           for three- (3) years or, in case of a vacancy, any portion of an unexpired term, so arranged that the term of office of as
  25|            nearly an equal number of members as is possible shall expire each year. The above Term of Appointment shall be
  26|            effective with the appointments by the Board of Selectmen commencing in fiscal year 2009;" and deleting the existing
  27|            Article 4, Section 3, Term Limits; and deleting the existing Article 5, Section 1, Regular Meetings, and replacing it with
  28|           "Regular meetings will be held as determined by the PACC with the time and place to be posted at Town Hall; and
  29|            deleting the existing Article 5, Section 4, Open Meeting Law, and replacing it with "All meetings shall be posted and
  30|           conducted in compliance with the provisions of MGL Chapter 39, Sections 23, 23A and 23B. The meeting agenda shall
  31|            be posted in accordance with the Town Charter;" and deleting the existing Article 7, Amendments, and replacing it with
  32|           "The PACC may consider amendments or alterations to these bylaws at either a regular or special PACC meeting. In
  33|            either case, notification of the meeting and its purpose shall be given fourteen-(14) days prior to assembly. The notice
  34|           shall include a summary of the proposed amendment, the full text of the proposed amendment or alteration, and a
  35|           statement of the purpose of the proposed change. The proposed amendment must be approved by a majority vote
  36|           of PACC. It will then be sent to the Board of Selectmen for insertion in the warrant and be submitted to the next Town
  37|           Meeting for approval. This protocol applies only to the PACC's ability to initiate changes to these bylaws, and is not
  38|           intended to usurp the rights given to registered voters of the Town specified in MG Chapter 39, Section 10;" and delete
  39|           the existing Article 9, Section 2, Possible Conflict, with "if any part of the policies and procedures adopted by the PACC
  40|           is in conflict with municipal bylaws, those of the municipality shall have preference." [Submitted by the Public Access
  41|           Cable Committee] Board of Selectmen recommend approval. Finance Committee reports no direct financial impact.
  42|           VOTED UNANIMOUSLY
  43|           Adjourned at 5:11 PM

   1|               SPECIAL TOWN MEETING WARRANT
   2|                September 25, 2012
   3|                The special town meeting was called to order at 7:00 P.M. in the Lunenburg High School Auditorium by the Moderator Timothy
   4|               W. Murphy. A quorum was declared present, the pledge of allegiance was recited and groundrules were given for conduct and
   5|                then action was taken on article 1.
   6|                ARTICLE 1. To see if the Town will vote to authorize the Board of Selectmen to enter into alternative energy power purchase
   7|               and/or net metering credit purchase agreements, including solar and wind energy and related net electricity metering credits,
   8|               for terms of more than three years, on such terms and conditions as the Board of Selectmen deem in the best interest of the
   9|               Town; and to authorize the Board of Selectmen to take all actions necessary to administer and implement such agreements; or
  10|               take any action relative thereto. Submitted by the Board of Selectmen Board of Selectmen and Finance Committee
  11|               recommend approval. VOTED that the Town authorize the Board of Selectmen to enter into alternative energy power purchase
  12|               and/or net metering credit purchase agreements, including solar energy and related net electricity metering credits, for terms
  13|               of more than three years, on such terms and conditions as the Board of Selectmen deem in the best interest of the Town; and
  14|               to authorize the Board of Selectmen to take all actions necessary to administer and implement such agreements.
  15|               ARTICLE 2. To see if the Town will vote to approve an Agreement for Payment in Lieu of Taxes (PILOT) pursuant to the
  16|               provisions of MGL Chapter 59, Section 38H(b), and Chapter164, Section 1, or any other enabling authority, for a certain sum
  17|               and a certain term of years, between the Town and the property owner for real property and personal property relating to a
  18|                Renewable Energy Generation Facility on the land located at 265 Pleasant Street and shown on Assessors' Map 097.0 as
  19|               Parcel(s) 0090; or take any action relative thereto. Submitted by the Board of Selectmen. Board of Selectmen and Finance
  20|               Committee recommend approval. VOTED that the Town approve an Agreement for Payment in Lieu of Taxes (PILOT) pursuant
  21|               to the provisions of MGL Chapter 59, Section 38H (b), and Chapter 164, Section 1, or any other enabling authority for $18,818.47
  22|               per MW for Personal Property plus an amount to be determined for Real Estate value for a period of twenty-(20) years, between
  23|               the Town and the property owner for real property and personal property relating to a Renewable Energy Generation Facility on
  24|               the land located at 265 Pleasant Street and shown on Assessors' Map 097.0 as Parcel 0090, provided however, that no such
  25|               agreement be executed unless and until the Board of Selectmen has entered into a Power Purchase Agreement for facility, and
  26|               authorize the Board of Selectmen to take all actions necessary to implement and administer such agreement.
  27|               ARTICLE 3. To see if the Town will vote to approve an Agreement for Payment in Lieu of Taxes (PILOT) pursuant to the
  28|               provisions of MGL Chapter 59, Section 38H(b), and Chapter164, Section 1, or any other enabling authority, for a certain sum
  29|               and a certain term of years, between the Town and the property owner for real property and personal property relating to a
  30|               Renewable Energy Generation Facility on the land located at 651 Chase Road and shown on Assessors' Map 21 as Parcel 2; or
  31|               take any action relative thereto.         Submitted by the Board of Selectmen. Board of Selectmen and Finance Committee
  32|               recommend approval. VOTED that the Town approve an Agreement for Payment in Lieu of Taxes (PILOT) pursuant to the
  33|               provisions of MGL Chapter 59, Section 38H (b), and Chapter 164, Section 1, or any other enabling authority for $18,818.47 per
  34|               MW for Personal Property plus an amount to be determined for Real Estate value for a period of twenty-(20) years, between the
  35|               Town and the property owner for real property and personal property relating to a Renewable Energy Generation Facility on the
  36|               land located at 651 Chase Road and shown on Assessors' Map 21 as Parcel 2, provided however, that no such agreement be
  37|               executed unless and until the Board of Selectmen has entered into a Power Purchase Agreement for facility, and authorize the
  38|               Board of Selectmen to take all actions necessary to implement and administer such agreement.
  39|               ARTICLE 4. To see if the Town will vote a Supplemental Appropriation of $35,598, to the amount appropriated at the 2012
  40|               Annual Town Meeting for the operation of the Lunenburg Public Library. Submitted by the Lunenburg Public Library Board of
  41|                           Board of Selectmen and Finance Committee recommend disapproval.                                Voted that the Town increase the

  12|                    QUESTION 1: Law Proposed Initiative Petition
  13|                    Do you approve of a law summarized below, on which no vote was taken by the Senate or
  14|                    the House of Representatives on or before May 1, 2012?
  15|                    WHAT YOUR VOTE WILL DO
  16|                    A YES VOTE would enact the proposed law requiring motor vehicle manufacturers to allow
  17|                    vehicle owners and independent repair facilities in Massachusetts to have access to the same
  18|                    vehicle diagnostic and repair information made available to the manufacturers' Massachusetts
  19|                    dealers and authorized repair facilities.
  20|                    A NO VOTE would make no change in existing laws.
